C07 residual checkpoint: scheduled barge terminal boundary

- Before repair, the exact shipped workflow `rtk proxy go run ./cmd/testtimeout --timeout 480s -- go test ./test/integration -count=1 -timeout 480s -v -run '^TestSessionCommand_LiveRecordDirAudioInTurnBargeInUsesActiveResponseBoundary$'` ran on code `0926d8771ead4a55f9b9af31aeb20cec39cf7b21` and exited 1 after the existing 10-second child deadline (`11.682s` wrapper; child `10.06s`). Its captured trace had two completed/dispatched turns and no third: `out:session.update`, `in:session.created`, `in:session.updated`, first append/commit/response.create, first response.created/audio, one response.cancel, cancelled response.done, second append/commit/response.create, second response.created/audio/response.done. The assertion reported `completed=2 dispatched=2 scheduled=3`; no third input effect was admitted.
- The cause was the barge scheduler waiting on `replayResponses`, which deliberately excludes the cancelled partial terminal. The first response terminal therefore never released the second wait, and the final finite gate also required three successful responses even though one scheduled input was legitimately cancelled. A terminal wake could additionally race before `responseActive` was cleared, causing an extra cancellation.
- Commit `3a09e4f88d32bb45c50544877836e42a7556cdd6` adds a barge-only response-boundary wait that counts owned cancellation terminals, waits until response state is settled, and makes scheduled finite completion use all observed response terminals while preserving `replayResponses` for successful replay semantics. The existing cancellation/stale-output boundary remains asserted; no broad suppression or timeout change was made.
- After repair, the exact same shipped workflow on `3a09e4f88d32bb45c50544877836e42a7556cdd6` passed (`11.825s`). Its existing assertions proved three non-empty input appends, three commits, three response.create events, exactly one response.cancel, three response.done terminals, response.created < cancel < second append < second response.done < third append, replacement and third response audio observed, and stale post-cancel audio absent. Focused runtime tests `TestInterruptedFiniteResponseDoesNotFinishBeforeReplacement`, `TestBargeCaptureWaitsOnCancelledResponseBoundary`, and `TestScheduledBargeCompletionCountsCancelledTerminal` passed; `go-agent-runtime/services/session/internal/live` passed in full.

C07 residual checkpoint: interleaved model/tool delta recovery

- Before repair, the focused race gate on the clean candidate `2a190e88e9c31b00632d32880a6fd1e3f4c9b512` repeatedly failed `TestS2SLiveBargeInOutstandingToolCallThroughCLI` while the image and active-response-boundary cases passed. The failure reported `input-2` without a commit or user turn, missing responses 2/3, and an unresolved `call-tool-barge-in`; temporary dispatch tracing showed only `[AUDIO.DELTA MESSAGE.END AUDIO.DELTA]` reached the session sink. Tool-forwarder tracing showed a failed run reconstructed `1` tool delta into `0` messages, while successful runs reconstructed the full tool batch and sent the result plus continuation.
- The causal boundary was `GlobalOrdering.UpdateWorldHistory`: a model delta arriving after tool `MESSAGE.START` used the model window index to overwrite/truncate already committed tool deltas. The later `MESSAGE.END` therefore could not reconstruct a `ToolCallID`, so the tool-result forwarder had no result to admit. The gateway response-intent experiment was removed because the failure remained with it and the end-to-end trace placed the loss earlier in the loop.
- Commit `4ca95b0a549803ff5a48783ba137f9dab8fb8f89` inserts model deltas before an already appended tool batch, shifts the active tool start index, and removes only stale model entries instead of truncating non-model history. C07 recovery checkpoint: settle raw audio commit and post-EOF response

- The remaining shipped duplex loss was traced past provider capture finalization. Raw `--audio-in -` admits PCM through the ordered session ingress, while the OpenAI/Grok provider sessions previously reported control success at local queue admission. A finite audio commit could therefore be acknowledged while its append/commit was still queued or while an audio response intent was deferred behind an active response. The runtime then stopped before the provider observed the final speech frame.
- The repair adds the optional `messages.SessionOutboundFlusher` capability. Provider outbound entries are tracked before queue admission and completed only after websocket writes settle; OpenAI also exposes deferred audio-intent settlement so a commit control waits through response-intent dispatch. The ordered live adapter waits on this capability before acknowledging `LiveControlAudioCommit`, and the recording session wrapper forwards it. `TestFlushOutboundWaitsForWebSocketWrite` and `TestRealtimeSession_FlushOutboundWaitsForDeferredAudioCommit` cover both queue and deferred-intent races.
- A second lifecycle repair records a post-EOF response target for an active continuous capture source. If an earlier VAD response already completed before stdin EOF, capture completion now requires one additional successful response instead of reusing that prior count. `TestCaptureCompletionWaitsForResponseAfterContinuousEOF` proves the boundary and preserves the no-source late-completion cases.
- The explicit `--wait-for-close` host policy now overrides the ordinary finite audio/output policy and has a focused assembly regression. This does not change the shipped duplex default; that fixture intentionally remains finite and is covered by the post-EOF target.

C18 public completion-forgery repair checkpoint

- The canonical task/review inbox was reread after the latest CI rejection. The
  unresolved review37 finding was the exported `StrictPreparedBuilder.Build`
  callback at `go-agent-runtime/services/replay/service.go:113-140`, which let
  an external consumer return nil from an arbitrary validator without consuming
  wire or tool evidence. The callback-bearing construction path is removed.
- `StrictPreparedBuilder.Build` now creates an opaque operation tracker around
  the public dialer and tool contracts; it has no caller-supplied validator and
  completion requires one successful dial plus the exact expected wire/tool
  operation counts. Transport/tool failures remain completion failures. The
  strict implementation no longer carries duplicate dead final validators.
  The external GOWORK=off regression checks the public method signature, public
  composite-literal forgery, and an unconsumed builder result; prior nil-context,
  exact packet/tool, integrity, and negative controls remain intact.
- Focused runtime replay normal/race each pass `77` tests in four packages;
